### G100001017956 / Elm Road power station / Unit 1, timepoint 2

### G100000401956 / Combined Locks energy center / CC1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed)*
- about: validation
- question: Is there any document stating Backup Power for Combined Locks CC1?
- finding: The EIA-860M table for September 2026 has no backup power field. The Wisconsin Department of Natural Resources permit fact sheet describes the cogeneration turbine as burning natural gas for the mill, which points to routine rather than backup use.
- recommendation: Leave Backup Power as no. No document that states it was found, so it stays without a source.
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Where I looked: the EIA-860M table at https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx, a web search for the plant, and the Department of Natural Resources fact sheet https://dnr.wisconsin.gov/sites/default/files/topic/Wastewater/pn/McKinleyPaper-FS.pdf, which is gone from its live address but saved at https://web.archive.org/web/20250915050421/https://dnr.wisconsin.gov/sites/default/files/topic/Wastewater/pn/McKinleyPaper-FS.pdf. The WARP permit harvest found no facility matched to this plant.

### G100000414276 / Diesel Generators power station / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed)*
- about: other
- question: Is the Diesel Generators unit in Madison a backup power unit?
- finding: No document I found states the Backup Power value for this unit. The Madison Gas and Electric page at https://mge.com/about-mge/power-plants/power-sources did not contain the words backup generators when checked, so it could not be cited. EIA lists the unit as an operating internal combustion engine with very low output.
- recommendation: Leave Backup Power as no and leave its source empty. Ask the Madison Gas and Electric contact whether the units serve customer backup or utility peaking.
- notes: I searched the web for Madison Gas and Electric diesel generators and read the EIA-860M table for September 2026. A search summary said the company owns about 60 MW of backup generators at business customer sites, but I could not find that sentence on the company page, so it is not a source.

- notes: Where I looked:
- I read the full text of the We Energies application to the Public Service Commission of Wisconsin at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=566031 and searched it for 'carbon capture' and 'CCS'. The only hits are on pages 3-22 and 3-23, in a summary of the federal power plant carbon rule. Nothing says Elm Road has or plans carbon capture.
- The EIA-860M table for September 2026 at https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx has no carbon capture column for plant 56068.
- The same finding applies to unit 1, timepoint 1, which the database does not flag.

### G100000409403 / Nemadji Trail energy center / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed)*
- about: validation
- question: Is there a document that states Backup Power for the Nemadji Trail unit?
- finding: I searched the owners' cancellation letter, the news reports and the power-technology.com profile. None says anything about backup power. The plant was a grid power station, not a data center.
- recommendation: Leave Backup Power as no. I looked in the owners' letter and news coverage and found no statement either way.
  - links: https://www.wpr.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/viewdoc.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.powermag.com/groups-cancel-1-billion-gas-fired-power-plant-project-in-wisconsin/ (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Sources opened: https://www.wpr.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/viewdoc.pdf and https://www.powermag.com/groups-cancel-1-billion-gas-fired-power-plant-project-in-wisconsin/.

### G100001018444 / Weston Power Plant / Unit 4, timepoint 2
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed), 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only))*
- about: validation
- question: Is Backup Power for Weston unit 4 really no?
- finding: I read the Public Service Commission application in docket 5-BS-293 and the 2025 and 2026 WEC investor updates. None says the unit is backup power.
- recommendation: Leave Backup Power as no. Coal becomes a backup fuel, but the unit itself is not backup power.
  - links: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=566895 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.wpr.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/09-2026-September-Final-1.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)

### G100001094209 / Forest Junction Energy Center / 1
*change to an existing unit; checklist rows 25 (Location data for every entry), 42 (Exact location for approximate operating units (optional))*
**Action:** Set Latitude of all 5 units of Forest Junction Energy Center to 44.23038, Longitude to -88.11715 and Location accuracy to exact. They now say 44.1880000, -88.0520000 and approximate, about 7.0 km away. Add the 3 links below to the Location Data Source box on each unit. Keep the links already there.
- `Latitude`: `44.1880000` -> `44.23038`
- `Longitude`: `-88.0520000` -> `-88.11715`
- `Location accuracy`: `approximate` -> `exact`
- location now: 44.1880000, -88.0520000 (approximate) [satellite](https://www.google.com/maps/@?api=1&map_action=map&center=44.18800,-88.05200&zoom=17&basemap=satellite) [pin](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=44.18800,-88.05200)
- location proposed: 44.23038, -88.11715 (exact) [satellite](https://www.google.com/maps/@?api=1&map_action=map&center=44.23038,-88.11715&zoom=17&basemap=satellite) [pin](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=44.23038,-88.11715)
- moved: 7.0 km
- method: georeferenced from a map in a document
- map render: [staging/maps/L100001083378.jpg](staging/maps/L100001083378.jpg)
  - control point: Holmes Road meets Schmidt Road at pixel 173,911 is 44.226727, -88.134137
  - control point: Highway 57 crosses Schmidt Road and County Road K at pixel 237,911 is 44.226715, -88.133168
  - control point: Holmes Road south leg meets County Road K at pixel 302,911 is 44.22671, -88.132162
  - control point: Highway 57 at the bend north of the site at pixel 512,154 is 44.23498, -88.12871
  - control point: Highway 57 halfway up the west edge at pixel 350,600 is 44.23012, -88.13134
- fit error: 0.6 m
  - Location Data Source: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=617761 (ok=True, contains_value=True)
  - Location Data Source: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=617707 (ok=True, contains_value=True)
  - Location Data Source: https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=44.23038,-88.11715 (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- confidence: high (independent: False)
- notes: This places the planned plant on the developer's own site map and checks it against satellite imagery. Where the site map is: - Open the Detailed Site Layout Map Book at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=617761 . It is Appendix A of the application in Wisconsin Public Service Commission docket 5-CE-162, received November 6, 2026. Go to PDF page 2, titled 'Detailed Site Layout Map Book: Proposed Site' for the 'Forest Junction & Union Depot Energy Centers' in Calumet County. - The page has no text layer, so I read it by eye. The outline marked 'Proposed Site Footprint' in the legend sits just north of County Road K, labeled 'Cth K', and east of Highway 57. Inside it are the plant pads and the site roads. How the point was found: - I tied the map to five road points from OpenStreetMap. They are where Holmes Road meets Schmidt Road, where Highway 57 crosses Schmidt Road, where the south part of Holmes Road meets County Road K, and two points on Highway 57 north of the road. - The map's own scale bar of 600 feet agrees with these points. The fit error is under 1 meter. - The middle of the outlined footprint is at 44.23038, -88.11715. Pin: https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=44.23038,-88.11715 . What the satellite picture shows: - A large farm field directly north of County Road K, with a curving drainage swale and a farmstead at its southwest corner. The map book shows the same field and farmstead. Nothing is built yet, which fits a planned plant. The imagery date is unknown. Other checks: - The application at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=617707 says on PDF page 20, section 2.1.1, 'The Proposed Site is located in Section 04, Township 20 North, Range 20 East' in the Town of Brillion. The point is inside that section. - The first look put the point at 44.22950, -88.11800 from wetland sampling points. The new point is about 120 m from it, so both looks agree. - The new point is about 7.0 km north and west of the point in the database, 44.18800, -88.05200. - The map book shows one footprint for both Forest Junction and Union Depot. The two plants share the site, so the same point fits both. The accuracy becomes exact. The developer's own site map in the application was tied to known road points with an error under 1 meter, and the planned site's field is seen in satellite imagery at that point. See the Latitude note for where to find each part. This is one of five 240.6 MW GE 7F.05 simple cycle turbines proposed at Forest Junction. The filing names no operator, and Latest Activity is left alone because the project filed its application on November 6, 2026. The georeferenced site map and the first look's wetland sampling points agree within about 120 m, so no further look is needed.

### G100001102105 / Union Depot Energy Center / 1
*change to an existing unit; checklist rows 25 (Location data for every entry), 42 (Exact location for approximate operating units (optional))*
**Action:** Set Latitude of unit 1 (G100001102105) to 44.23250, Longitude to -88.11630 and Location accuracy to exact. They now say 44.190, -88.058 and approximate, about 6.6 km away. Add the 4 links below to the Location Data Source box. Keep the links already there.
- `Latitude`: `44.190` -> `44.23250`
- `Longitude`: `-88.058` -> `-88.11630`
- `Location accuracy`: `approximate` -> `exact`
- location now: 44.190, -88.058 (approximate) [satellite](https://www.google.com/maps/@?api=1&map_action=map&center=44.19000,-88.05800&zoom=17&basemap=satellite) [pin](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=44.19000,-88.05800)
- location proposed: 44.23250, -88.11630 (exact) [satellite](https://www.google.com/maps/@?api=1&map_action=map&center=44.23250,-88.11630&zoom=17&basemap=satellite) [pin](https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=44.23250,-88.11630)
- moved: 6.6 km
- method: published
  - Location Data Source: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=617707 (ok=True, contains_value=True)
  - Location Data Source: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=617796 (ok=True, contains_value=True)
  - Location Data Source: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=617756 (ok=True, contains_value=True)
  - Location Data Source: https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=44.23250,-88.11630 (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- confidence: high (independent: False)
- notes: This sets the point for Union Depot Energy Center. The new point is 44.23250, -88.11630. It is the combined cycle power block inside the site fence that the developer drew in its application to the Public Service Commission of Wisconsin. Where the site is: - The joint application for a Certificate of Public Convenience and Necessity, filed November 6, 2026 in docket 5-CE-162, is at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=617707. On page 20 of the file, section 7.11.1, it says 'The Proposed Site is located in Section 04, Township 20 North, Range 20 East. The permanent footprint of the Proposed Site is approximately 85 acres.' - Section 7.11.2 on the same page says construction traffic will enter the site from County Road K. Where the plant sits inside the site: - Appendix L of the application is a zip file of mapping data, at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=617796. Its layer 'Proposed_Site_Footprint' is one shape of 84.8 acres. It runs from 44.22705 to 44.23355 north and from -88.12044 to -88.11390 east. Its layer 'Proposed_Site_Fence_Line' is the fenced area, from 44.22763 to 44.23347 north and from -88.11894 to -88.11426 east. - The site layout drawing in Appendix A, 'Proposed Site GA', is at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=617756. Page 2 of the file is drawing GA001, revision J, titled 'Forest Junction Energy Center Union Depot Energy Center General Arrangement'. It is drawn on an air photo. The five simple cycle turbines of Forest Junction fill the middle and south of the fence. The one combined cycle unit of Union Depot is at the north end, with its gas turbine, heat recovery steam generator, stack, steam turbine and air cooled condenser fans. - I laid the mapping layers over a satellite picture. The footprint matches the farm field north of County Road K, and the stormwater pond and the drainage swale line up with the drawing. Read against the pond and the fence, the center of the combined cycle block falls at about 44.23250, -88.11630. The imagery date is unknown, and the field is still bare farmland. - The point is shown on a map at https://www.google.com/maps/search/?api=1&query=44.23250,-88.11630. Why the accuracy is exact: - The point rests on the site footprint coordinates filed with the application, and the plant's own block is placed from the developer's site drawing. That is the manual's route for coordinates in a permit application or a similar document. How far it moves: - The point moves about 6.6 km from GEM's point 44.19000, -88.05800, which is near Brillion. It is about 500 m from the middle of the wetland survey area that the first look used. The project is still pre-construction, with construction planned to start in Q4 2027 and finish in Q4 2031. The plan names a GE 7HA.03 turbine and 750 MW, matching GEM. No operator or combined heat and power statement was found in the plan. The move is over 2 km, but it rests on the applicant's own filed site footprint and site drawing, which both agree with the satellite picture, so no further check is needed.

### G100001071185 / Red Oak Ridge Energy Center / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 11 (Every unit has a unit name)*
- about: identity
- question: Which EIA generator ids belong to the five Red Oak Ridge units?
- finding: The EIA-860M table for September 2026 lists five generators at plant 70453, named CT4, CT5, RDOR1, RDOR2 and RDOR3, each 242 MW and planned for December 2029. CT4 and CT5 are marked as pending regulatory approval and not under construction, and the other three as planned with approvals not yet started.
- recommendation: Type EIA plant 70453 into Other IDs for the plant. Map the five generators to units 1 to 5 in order, since the units are identical, and add each generator id to Other IDs for its unit.
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Open the EIA-860M workbook at https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx, sheet Planned, and filter Plant ID 70453. Rows list Entity Name 'Invenergy Services LLC' and Plant Name 'Red Oak Ridge Energy Center LLC'. Technology reads 'Natural Gas Fired Combustion Turbine' and Planned Operation Year reads 2029. No document found ties a particular generator id to a particular GEM unit. EIA shows a planned operating year of 2029, while GEM shows a start year of 2030.

The exact wording in the table is 'Regulatory approvals pending. Not under construction' for CT4 and CT5, and 'Planned for installation, but regulatory approvals not initiated' for the other three.

- finding: The approved site is the Proposed Site, about 25 acres of farm field in Section 13, Township 2 North, Range 21 East, in the Town of Paris. GEM's point sits on a farm field beside Interstate 94, with a logistics warehouse to the north and an asphalt plant to the south, which matches the application.
- recommendation: Leave the point as it is and leave City as Somers, since the parcels sit in the Village of Somers growth area. The EIA point is about 870 meters to the west and is less accurate.

It says the Proposed Project Area consists of three parcels, and that 'Parcel 134-0101 is located in the Village of Somers Growth Area', while the other two sit in the City of Kenosha Growth Area. It also says the site is 'adjacent to an asphalt manufacturing plant, a logistics warehouse, and U.S. Interstate 41 / U.S. Interstate 94'. I looked at a satellite picture of GEM's point at 42.63143, -87.95560 and saw all three features. The imagery date is unknown. The Commission page says Town of Paris, which is the township.

- notes: This is the Public Service Commission of Wisconsin decision on the Red Oak Ridge Energy Center, docket 9837-CE-100. Decision: - Open the minutes of the October 15, 2026 open meeting at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=615912 and find the entry headed 9837-CE-100, on pages 7 and 8. It says 'The Commission granted a CPCN for the proposed project, with conditions.' CPCN means Certificate of Public Convenience and Necessity. - The same entry says the Commission 'directed the Division of Energy Regulation and Analysis to draft an order consistent with its discussion.' The minutes name three commissioners present, Chairperson Strand, Commissioner Nieto and Commissioner Hawkins. They do not record a vote count. Conditions: - Open the final decision matrix dated October 6, 2026 at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=614384 and go to Issue 7, the standard conditions. Condition a says the applicant may build at 'an installed capacity of no greater than 1,210 MW alternating current'. Written order: - The Commission's filing list for the docket, harvested on November 17, 2026, shows no written final decision yet. The written order should be added to Status Detail when it is posted. The status stays pre-construction. The Commission granted the certificate on October 15, 2026 but no start of construction was found, and a certificate only allows construction. Start year 2030 is consistent with the We Energies application at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=568295, which says full commercial operation by June 30, 2030, so it was left alone. The project is moving, so Latest Activity was left alone. No operator was named in any document. Number of engines and capacity per engine are not filled because these are frame turbines.

It says 'The Commission granted a CPCN for the proposed project, with conditions.' CPCN means Certificate of Public Convenience and Necessity. - The same entry says the Commission 'directed the Division of Energy Regulation and Analysis to draft an order consistent with its discussion.' The minutes name three commissioners present, Chairperson Strand, Commissioner Nieto and Commissioner Hawkins. They do not record a vote count. Conditions: - Open the final decision matrix dated October 6, 2026 at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=614384 and go to Issue 7, the standard conditions. Condition a says the applicant may build at 'an installed capacity of no greater than 1,210 MW alternating current'. Written order: - The Commission's filing list for the docket, harvested on November 17, 2026, shows no written final decision yet. The written order should be added to Status Detail when it is posted. The status stays pre-construction. The Commission granted the certificate on October 15, 2026 but no start of construction was found, and a certificate only allows construction. Start year 2030 is consistent with the We Energies application at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=568295, which says full commercial operation by June 30, 2030, so it was left alone. The project is moving, so Latest Activity was left alone. No operator was named in any document. Number of engines and capacity per engine are not filled because these are frame turbines.

- confidence: high (independent: False)
- notes: The project application for docket 9837-CE-100 gives 242 MW for each gas turbine generator, so the capacity stays at 242 MW. Capacity: - Open the public copy of the application at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=572462 and search for section 1.1.6, 'Identify each proposed generating unit'. It says 'Each gas turbine generator ("GTG") will have an individual electrical generating capacity of approximately 242 MW, for a total installed capacity of approximately 1,210 MW.' - The same section says the most that can be delivered to the grid is 1,186 MW, or about 237 MW per unit. ### G100001102201 / Vantage Lighthouse (WI-1) Data Center power station / 1
*change to an existing unit; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 39 (Gas-powered data centers searched, captive data entered (US only)), 49 (Construction to operating checked)*
**Action:** Leave Capacity (MW) of unit 1 (G100001102201) as 87. It is still correct. Add the link below to the Capacity Data Source box. Keep the links already there.
- `Capacity (MW)`: `87` -> `87`
  - Capacity Data Source: https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/warp_ext/AM_DownloadObject.aspx?id=1275115 (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- confidence: high (independent: False)
- notes: Capacity: - Open the final air permit at https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/warp_ext/AM_DownloadObject.aspx?id=1275115 and find the equipment table. It lists three groups of diesel emergency engines. Processes P01 to P15 are 15 engines with maximum ratings of 1,500 kW. Processes P16 to P33 are 18 engines at 1,750 kW. Processes P34 to P45 are 12 engines at 2,750 kW. - That sums to 22.5 plus 31.5 plus 33.0, which is 87.0 MW. It matches the 87 MW GEM already shows. Why the permit harvest showed 46 engines and 97.5 MW: - The harvest counted from the application. The application at https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/warp_ext/AM_DownloadObject.aspx?id=1235892 says 'up to forty-five (45) diesel emergency generators' in its project description. The 46 and 97.5 MW figures are a reading error in the harvest, not a different fleet. The final permit confirms 45 diesel engines and 87.0 MW, matching GEM. The harvest's 46 engines and 97.5 MW is a reading error. The permit record lists no permit for another phase. No new owner or operator document was found beyond the applicant name Vantage Data Centers Management Company, LLC.

- notes: Open the EIA-860M table for September 2026 at https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx, sheet Operating, plant 4078 Weston, generator 4. The row reads status OP, nameplate 595.0 MW, Conventional Steam Coal, operating year 2008. The Public Service Commission application at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=566895 also says 'nameplate generating capacity of 595 MW'.

### Columbia Energy Center
*watch item at an existing plant; checklist rows 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only))*
- what to watch: The state's draft energy assessment lists a gas conversion of both Columbia coal units in 2030, but Alliant says coal continues at least through 2030 and Madison Gas and Electric says it is only exploring the switch. No docket, permit or engineering plan exists yet.
- check again by: 2027-06
  - links: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=595734 (ok=True, contains_value=True)
  - links: https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0000352541/000035254126000047/lnt-20260930.htm (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- notes: The Public Service Commission draft Strategic Energy Assessment of June 2026, Table 2-2, lists fuel switch retrofits for Columbia 1 and 2 at 566 and 565 MW in 2030. Alliant's quarterly report for the third quarter of 2026 says Wisconsin Power and Light plans to keep coal operations at Columbia Units 1 and 2 at least through 2030. Madison Gas and Electric's 2025 annual report says it is exploring converting the Columbia Energy Center from coal to natural gas. GEM has Columbia only as a coal plant. Recheck for a construction or conversion docket at the state utility commission and a state air permit application.

### G100001017956 / Elm Road power station / Unit 1, timepoint 2
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 19 (Missing or unknown owner searched)*
- about: Owner(s)
- question: Should the majority owner be Elm Road Generating Station Supercritical LLC?
- finding: The application says the units are majority-owned by Elm Road Generating Station Supercritical, LLC, with MGE and WPPI at 8.33% each. The WEC Energy Group 2025 annual report says its We Power subsidiary owns the rest and leases it to Wisconsin Electric, so Wisconsin Electric is the lessee and operator.
- recommendation: Change the 83% owner from Wisconsin Electric Power Co to Elm Road Generating Station Supercritical LLC, and name We Power as its parent if the entity list has it.
  - links: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=566031 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/783325/000078332526000018/wec-20251231.htm (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Open https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=566031 and read section 6.2.1, Previous and Current Land Use and Ownership. In the WEC Energy Group 2025 annual report at https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/783325/000078332526000018/wec-20251231.htm, the We Power section says two unaffiliated entities own about 17% of Elm Road units 1 and 2, and We Power's share is leased to Wisconsin Electric under 30-year leases. The report does not name the LLC, so the link between We Power and the LLC is inferred from the application.

### G100001094209 / Forest Junction Energy Center / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 19 (Missing or unknown owner searched)*
- about: Owner(s)
- question: Should the owner be Forest Junction Energy Center LLC instead of Invenergy LLC?
- finding: The application says Forest Junction Energy Center LLC is a subsidiary of Invenergy Thermal Development Holdings LLC. The database shows Invenergy LLC at 100 percent.
- recommendation: Change the owner on all five units to Forest Junction Energy Center LLC [100%] if the reviewer agrees that the project company is the holder. Leave it as is if Invenergy LLC is the entity the tracker uses for Invenergy projects.

The first paragraph of the application says the two applicants are 'subsidiaries of Invenergy Thermal Development Holdings LLC'. Page 14 also says the project company's affiliate signed a procurement agreement with Wisconsin Electric Power Company, but no sale of the plant is announced. We Energies has not filed to buy the plant, so no pending acquisition is recorded. The entity lookup for Forest Junction Energy Center LLC found nothing in the local list. The name Invenergy exists already as an entity.

### G100001054548 / Foundry Ridge Energy Center / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry)*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: Should capacity stay at 108 MW per unit when We Energies says 270 MW in total?
- finding: The commission approved up to 324 MW at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=615911. The We Energies application at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=568288, Description of Facilities, says up to 270 MW nameplate for the three GE 7E.03 turbines.
- recommendation: Leave 108 MW per unit, because 324 MW is the installed size the commission approved and EIA lists 108 MW each. Ask the project manager if the 270 MW figure should be tracked.

### G100000414139 / French Island power station / 3
*question about existing data; checklist rows 21 (Planned retirements searched), 43 (Ownership changes for operating units reviewed (optional)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
- about: Planned retire
- question: Does Xcel's plan to run French Island waste-to-energy units to 2040 change the 2030 retirement date?
- finding: Xcel's 2024 Minnesota resource plan says the French Island waste-to-energy plant will run to 2040, while EIA lists all four generators at the site, including the oil turbines, retiring in June 2030. The plan does not mention the oil turbines.
- recommendation: Leave units 3 and 4 at 2030. Check the waste-to-energy units separately if GEM tracks them.
- notes: The plan is the filing 20242-203027-01 in Minnesota docket E002/RP-24-67, saved in scopes/us-wi/work/irp/xcel. On the PDF, the paragraph that starts 'Finally, all three of our renewable Refuse Derived Fuel' says French Island runs to 2040. I could not cite a public web address for it.

### G100001102182 / Microsoft Mount Pleasant Data Centers power station / Area 2 (MKE07-15)
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 39 (Gas-powered data centers searched, captive data entered (US only))*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: Should the Mount Pleasant fleet be raised from 672 MW to the permit's 722.5 MW?
- finding: GEM carries 146 + 39 + 39 engines at 3 MW each, 672 MW. The DNR preliminary determination for permit 25-MIN-113 lists 244 engines that sum to 722.5 MW, and the Aug 2026 application adds 6 half-megawatt and 9 one-megawatt Caterpillar units for Area 2.
- recommendation: Leave GEM's three rows as they are for now. Ask the project manager whether the one-megawatt, half-megawatt and Clarke fire pump units (which GEM does not carry) should be added as extra rows.
  - links: https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/warp_ext/AM_DownloadObject.aspx?id=1262690 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/warp_ext/AM_DownloadObject.aspx?id=1287957 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Where to look:
- Preliminary determination at https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/warp_ext/AM_DownloadObject.aspx?id=1262690, Source Description table. It lists Cummins C3000 D6e at 'up to 3,213' kW for each of 39 + 39 + 146 core engines, plus smaller Cummins 500DFEK, 1000DQFAD and Clarke fire pump units.
- Application at https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/warp_ext/AM_DownloadObject.aspx?id=1287957, Section 1.2 and Table 1, and Appendix B table 'Mount Pleasant Area Designations'. It lists Area 2 as 146 three-megawatt units, 9 one-megawatt units and 9 half-megawatt units.
- 39 x 3.213 MW is 125.3 MW against 117 MW in GEM. The permit figure is the largest rating allowed, so I made no change.
- The application also names Area East (136 acres) and a 150-acre parcel Microsoft is acquiring, which would be further fleets. Amalia Llano's note and the Mount Pleasant village agendas were not read separately.

### G100001071185 / Red Oak Ridge Energy Center / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry)*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: Should capacity stay 242 MW per unit given a We Energies filing that says 1,125 MW total?
- finding: The project application and the Commission decision use five GE 7F.05 turbines at 242 MW each, 1,210 MW total. The We Energies purchase application says 'up to 1,125 MW (nameplate capacity) using five GE 7E.05 gas turbines', which is 225 MW each.
- recommendation: Leave capacity at 242 MW and the model at 7F.05 for now. Ask the project manager to check whether We Energies has received a changed turbine plan, and revisit when the written Commission order is posted.
  - links: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=568295 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=572462 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=614384 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=615912 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Open the We Energies application filed on November 20, 2025 at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=568295 and read the first page of the introduction. It says 'a high quality, natural gas- fueled 1,125 MW (nameplate capacity) simple-cycle combustion turbine'. Page 2 repeats it with 'five GE 7E.05 gas turbines'. The developer's own application at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=572462, section 1.1.6, says 'five GE 7F.05 gas turbines' at about 242 MW each. The Commission minutes at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=615912 and decision matrix at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=614384 both name 1,210 MW. The 1,186 MW figure on the Commission case page is the grid delivery limit in the application. The We Energies document is the older record of the purchase deal, and the gap may simply be an old or incorrect model name.

Backup Power no is confirmed by the Public Service Commission filing that treats the plant as a grid peaking plant.

It now says MGE Energy Inc [6%]; Wisconsin Power and Light Co [56%]; Rock Energy Cooperative [3%]; Central Wisconsin Electric Cooperative Inc [0%]; Wisconsin Electric Power Co [27%]; Adams-Columbia Electric Cooperative [4%]. Add the 2 links below to the Owners Data Source box. Keep the links already there.
- `Owner(s)`: `MGE Energy Inc [6%]; Wisconsin Power and Light Co [56%]; Rock Energy Cooperative [3%]; Central Wisconsin Electric Cooperative Inc [0%]; Wisconsin Electric Power Co [27%]; Adams-Columbia Electric Cooperative [4%]` -> `Wisconsin Power and Light Co [57%]; Wisconsin Electric Power Co [27%]; MGE Energy Inc [7%]; small shareholder(s) [9%]`
  - Owners Data Source: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=481060 (ok=True, contains_value=True)
  - Owners Data Source: https://web.archive.org/web/20250307014442/https://energycentral.com/news/alliant-energy-corporation-quarterly-report-quarter-ending-september-30-2024-form-10-q (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- confidence: high (independent: False)
- notes: Owners: - The database lists six owners. Our rules record the four largest and fold the rest into one line, and owners under 5 percent are grouped as small shareholders. So I shortened the list to Wisconsin Power and Light, Wisconsin Electric Power, MGE Energy and small shareholders. - Open the joint application to the Public Service Commission of Wisconsin filed October 2, 2023 (docket 5-AE-245) at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=481060. On page 7, the table just below the paragraph that starts 'Together, the units have a nameplate capacity of 727.2 MW' lists current ownership: Wisconsin Power and Light 412.2 MW at 56.6 percent, Wisconsin Electric 200 MW at 27.5 percent, MGE 50 MW at 6.9 percent, Adams-Columbia Electric Cooperative 32.6 MW at 4.5 percent, Rock Energy Cooperative 26.9 MW at 3.7 percent and Central Wisconsin Electric Cooperative 5.5 MW at 0.8 percent. - The three cooperatives are each under 5 percent and add up to 9.0 percent, which is the small shareholder line. I rounded 27.5 percent down to 27 so the shares total 100. - The Alliant Energy quarterly report for September 30, 2024 is gone from its old address at energycentral.com. A saved copy is cited instead at https://web.archive.org/web/20250307014442/https://energycentral.com/news/alliant-energy-corporation-quarterly-report-quarter-ending-september-30-2024-form-10-q . In its notes to the financial statements it says 'WPL's undivided current ownership interest in West Riverside is 56.6%', which matches. The old address is kept in the source box, because sources are never removed. - The 2023 transfer in that docket was a proposal, so I did not change any share for it.

- notes: The October 15, 2026 decision covers the certificate only, not the purchase.

### G100000401778 / LSP-Whitewater power station / CC1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 19 (Missing or unknown owner searched), 43 (Ownership changes for operating units reviewed (optional)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
- about: Owner(s)
- question: Should the owners of LSP-Whitewater change to the We Energies companies?
- finding: A 2022 commission decision approved a 50-50 purchase by Wisconsin Electric Power Company and Wisconsin Public Service Corporation, and a We Energies fact sheet says the plant was bought in 2023. EIA lists Wisconsin Electric Power Co.
- recommendation: Confirm the closing and the shares in the 2023 utility filings, then replace the four former owners with the buyers. Until then leave the owner list as it is.
  - links: https://www.we-energies.com/company/pdf/whitewater-generating-station.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=455168 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: The owner list for this plant may be out of date.

What the sources say:
- The Public Service Commission of Wisconsin final decision at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=455168 (docket 5-BS-264, decision dated November 2022, page 1) says it grants Wisconsin Electric Power Company and Wisconsin Public Service Corporation authority to buy the LSP-Whitewater facility. The paragraph under 'Introduction' says 'each applicant would acquire 50 percent of the Whitewater facility's capacity'. I read the full decision at that address.
- A We Energies fact sheet at https://www.we-energies.com/company/pdf/whitewater-generating-station.pdf says on page 1, under 'Initial cost', that 'We Energies purchased the power plant from LSP Whitewater in 2023 for $75 million'. It does not name Wisconsin Public Service Corporation.
- The EIA-860M table for September 2026 at https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx lists plant 55011, generators CTG1 and STG1, under the entity Wisconsin Electric Power Co.

What was not found: a document that states the closing date or the final shares. The owner list in GEM still shows the former LSP-Whitewater partners.

### G100001071185 / Red Oak Ridge Energy Center / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 19 (Missing or unknown owner searched)*
- about: attribution

The company's letter of November 12, 2026 at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=618277 says the cost estimate rose from $1,769,801,000 to $2,311,427,000, that the application is still pending, and that '100% of ROREC Project is being subscribed to by the VLC', meaning a very large customer. Its name is not given in the public record. No source says the plant serves one site, so the captive fields stay blank.

- Add a Notes line saying the purchase is pending.

### G100000409403 / Nemadji Trail energy center / 1
*change to an existing unit; checklist rows 38 (GEM IDs matched to EIA-860M, EIP and Sierra Club (US only))*
**Action:** Add the entry below at the top of the Status Detail box of unit 1 (G100000409403), above the text already there. Keep the existing text below it, word for word. The source links are part of the entry, one per line. Do not add them to the Status Data Source box.

Project cancelled. On October 28, 2026 Dairyland Power Cooperative and South Shore Energy wrote to the Public Service Commission of Wisconsin to cancel both approvals, dockets 9698-CE-100 and 9698-CE-101. No construction had occurred.

https://www.wpr.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/viewdoc.pdf
https://www.powermag.com/groups-cancel-1-billion-gas-fired-power-plant-project-in-wisconsin/
- `Status Detail`: `ALQ42026: "Accordingly, the Owners respectfully request that the Commission acknowledge their relinquishment of all rights associated with the CPCNs, release them from any obligations associated with the CPCNs, and close Docket Nos. 9698-CE-100 and 9698-CE-101." Project officially cancelled in Oct. 28, 2026

NF 11/3/25: PSCW docket status https://psc.wi.gov/Pages/CommissionActions/CasePages/NemadjiTrailEnergyCenterProject.aspx

NF 6/18/25: Still stalled. No updates.

AL: plant withdrew air permit request in October 2024 due to "extended timeline of the federal permit process". One article says project is not scrapped, but this probably means it will be at least very delayed. Article says plant's fate "will be in limbo until owners decide whether to pursue new permits." Still facing strong opposition.  As of mid-October, owners had requested withdrawal of the plant's air permit, with plans to "determine when to re-apply based on project planning and permitting.”
https://www.newsfromthestates.com/article/superior-gas-plant-withdraws-permit-request-leaving-project-limbo
However, permit withdrawal has yet to be approved and finalized by the Department of Natural Resources (DNR)

NF 6/10/24: Strong opposition. In April 2024, Superior City Council has essentially denied local approvals. No source says they serve as backup for a data center.

### G100000403768 / Alliant Energy Neenah power station / CT01

The EIA-860M table for September 2026 (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx) still shows the old 185.5 MW nameplate.
- recommendation: Leave 220 MW on both units. Recheck when EIA publishes its next annual survey.

### G100000414268 / Germantown power station / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 38 (GEM IDs matched to EIA-860M, EIP and Sierra Club (US only))*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: Should Germantown capacities follow the EIA nameplate or the DNR permit ratings?
- finding: The EIA-860M table for September 2026 gives 71.2 MW for Units 1 to 4 and 105.9 MW for Unit 5. The 2019 Wisconsin DNR permit at https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/warp_ext/AM_DownloadObject.aspx?id=859072 says Units 1 to 4 total 224 MW and Unit 5 is 85 MW.
- recommendation: Use the EIA nameplate figures, 71.2 MW for each of Units 1 to 4 and 105.9 MW for Unit 5, because GEM records nameplate capacity.
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/warp_ext/AM_DownloadObject.aspx?id=859072 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: In the permit, go to the equipment list near the start of the permit. The line starts 'Eight Simple Cycle Combustion Turbines arranged in pairs' and ends 'total electrical capacity of 224 MW'. The Unit 5 line ends 'total electric capacity of 85 MW'. The permit ratings are lower than EIA's nameplate, which is likely a site rating at permit conditions. Each of Units 1 to 4 is a pair of Pratt and Whitney turbines, per a 2024 DNR application, but I could not verify that page with the link checker because its text is spaced out, so I did not report it.

### WPPI Energy planned gas capacity
*watch item, new to the tracker; checklist rows 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only)), 51 (Newly announced projects checked)*
- what to watch: WPPI Energy says it plans about 400 MW of new gas-fired capacity but names no site, unit or date beyond its planning horizon.
- check again by: 2027-06
  - links: https://wppienergy.org/powering-ahead/ (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- notes: WPPI Energy's Powering Ahead page says it plans about 400 MW of new gas-fired capacity. Its State of WPPI 2025 report, 27 pages, shows a chart of peak demand and capacity for 2026 to 2035 and names no gas unit. Searches of the state utility commission filing system, the state air permit system and the queue file found no WPPI gas project. Recheck WPPI's next annual report and the commission filing system.

### We Energies and Wisconsin Public Service unnamed gas additions 2031 to 2032
*watch item, new to the tracker; checklist rows 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only)), 51 (Newly announced projects checked)*
- what to watch: WEC Energy Group's capital plan counts more combustion turbine and engine capacity than its named projects add up to, and the state energy assessment lists Gas TBD rows with no site. No project or site is described.
- check again by: 2027-03
  - links: https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/783325/000078332526000109/a112026november.htm (ok=True, contains_value=True)
  - links: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=595734 (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- notes: WEC Energy Group's November 2026 investor presentation lists 3,540 MW of combustion turbines and engines in its capital plan. The named projects add to 2,623 MW: South Oak Creek 1,100 MW, Red Oak Ridge 1,125 MW, Foundry Ridge 270 MW and the Paris engines 128 MW. That leaves about 917 MW unnamed. The Public Service Commission draft Strategic Energy Assessment, Table B-8, lists Gas TBD rows of 28 MW in 2031 for Wisconsin Electric, 28 MW in 2031 for Wisconsin Public Service and 1,138 MW in 2032 for Wisconsin Electric, with no site. Recheck the commission filing system for new construction dockets under utility numbers 6630 and 6690 and the next investor update.

The federal monthly generator file for September 2026 (Operating sheet, plant 3991) lists two natural gas combustion turbines at Fitchburg in Dane County, generators 1 and 2, each 28.8 MW, 57.6 MW in all. Both have run since May 1973 and are owned by Madison Gas & Electric Co. The state air permit for the site at 5701 McKee Road says the turbines burn natural gas with distillate oil as the backup fuel. The coordinates come from the federal file and are left approximate, because nobody has checked the point on a satellite image.

- confidence: high (independent: )
- notes: GEM's only gas unit at Riverside is CC_set, 723 MW from 2020, which is the newer West Riverside block (federal plant 64020, shared owners). The older block is missing. The federal monthly generator file for September 2026 (Operating sheet, plant 55641 Riverside Energy Center) lists CTG1 198.9 MW, CTG2 198.9 MW and STG1 277.1 MW, operating since June 2004 and owned by Wisconsin Power & Light Co, 674.9 MW in all. The state air permit for the site describes four gas-fired combined cycle turbines in all and a project to raise the 2004 block's output from 625 MW by replacing its turbine parts. The Public Service Commission draft Strategic Energy Assessment, Table B-8, counts that as 156 MW of Riverside upgrades in 2028, and MISO request J3156 (100 MW, Townline Road, Rock County) is its grid request. Seven 2,500 kilowatt Caterpillar black start engines (17.5 MW in Alliant's 2025 annual report) also sit at the site and are below the unit threshold.

### G100001094209 / Forest Junction Energy Center / 1
*question about existing data*
- about: duplicate
- question: Is MISO queue entry J4014 at 650 MW part of Union Depot rather than Forest Junction?
- finding: The application says J4014 at 650 MW is the Union Depot queue position at the Forest Junction Substation. Forest Junction itself has a signed interconnection agreement for 1,203 MW.
- recommendation: Match J4014 to Union Depot and do not count it against the five Forest Junction units. Leave Forest Junction at five units of 240.6 MW.
  - links: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=617707 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Open https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=617707 and go to PDF page 18. It says 'Forest Junction has signed a Generator Interconnection Agreement with ATC and MISO for 1,203 MW' and 'Union Depot filed a MISO generator interconnection queue position (J4014)' for 650 MW. A second Union Depot entry, J4249 at 100 MW, is also named there. Union Depot Energy Center is its own plant record in this Wisconsin batch, so J4014 belongs there.

### G100001054548 / Foundry Ridge Energy Center / 1
*question about existing data*
- about: identity
- question: Is EIA plant 70360 the same project as Foundry Ridge Energy Center?
- finding: The EIA-860M table for September 2026 (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx) lists plant 70360, Foundry Ridge Energy Center LLC, Walworth County, with three 108 MW gas combustion turbines planned for 2028. That matches the 324 MW approved at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=615911.
- recommendation: Treat it as a match and do not add a new plant. Type EIA 70360 into Other IDs for the location, and generators FNDRD, FNRD2 and FNRD3 into Other IDs for units 1, 2 and 3.
  - links: https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFview/viewdoc.aspx?docid=615911 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Generator IDs are in the Planned sheet of the file. The plant is 3.6 km from GEM's point, which fits the commission's proposed site on the Darien Solar land.

### G100000409403 / Nemadji Trail energy center / 1
*question about existing data*
- about: identity
- question: Is EIA plant 8057 Superior Generation Energy Center the same project as Nemadji Trail?
- finding: The EIA-860M table for September 2026, sheet 'Canceled or Postponed', has plant code 8057, 'Superior Generation Energy Center', Douglas County, owner entity ALLETE, Inc., generator GEN1, 170 MW, natural gas fired combustion turbine, about 0.9 km from GEM's point. The 170 MW and single combustion turbine do not match the 625 MW combined cycle unit.
- recommendation: Do not link plant 8057 to this unit, since it looks like an older ALLETE proposal on the same site. Ask the project manager before typing any EIA code.
- notes: Plant code 8057, generator ID GEN1. Nemadji Trail has no EIA code. The file is https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx.

### G100000403762 / Paris (WI) power station / 1
*question about existing data*
- about: duplicate
- question: Are the Sierra Club Paris Internal Combustion rows already held as the Paris RICE power station?
- finding: The Sierra Club rows for units 5 to 11 describe the seven natural gas engines that We Energies is building in Kenosha County. GEM already holds them as the plant Paris RICE power station, in construction at 131.6 MW.
- recommendation: Add no units to Paris (WI) power station. Leave the existing Paris RICE power station record as the home of these engines.
  - links: https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/warp_ext/AM_DownloadObject.aspx?id=1279873 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Source:
- The Public Service Commission of Wisconsin docket 6630-CE-316 is titled 'Application of Wisconsin Electric Power Company for a Certificate of Public Convenience and Necessity to Construct and Operate the Paris Reciprocating Internal Combustion Engines Project, Consisting of Seven Natural Gas-Fired Reciprocating Internal Combustion Engines Generating up to 128 MW Total'. The title is on the docket search page for 6630-CE-316 on the commission's filing system at https://apps.psc.wi.gov/ERF/ERFsearch/.
- The engines sit at the Lakeshore Capacity Improvement Project regulator station in the Town of Paris, not on the grounds of the four turbines.

What I checked:
- The GEM export has a separate plant called Paris RICE power station with one unit, status construction, 131.6 MW.
- The 2026 state air permits 25-PSD-061 and 26-PSD-057 for the Paris Generating Station, read at https://apps.dnr.wi.gov/warp_ext/AM_DownloadObject.aspx?id=1279873, cover heaters and similar equipment, not new generating units.

I did not open the large engineering documents in the docket, so I did not compare the 128 MW title figure with the 131.6 MW in GEM. That figure belongs to the Paris RICE record, not to this plant.

### Riverside Energy Center
*question about existing data*
- about: scope
- question: Should the 2004 Riverside combined cycle block become its own plant, apart from the West Riverside block that GEM now holds under this plant?
- finding: The federal generator file lists two plants at the Beloit site: plant 55641 Riverside Energy Center (CTG1, CTG2 and STG1, 674.9 MW from 2004, owned only by Wisconsin Power and Light) and plant 64020 West Riverside Energy Center (CTG3, CTG4 and STG2, 723.2 MW from 2020, shared by six owners). GEM's plant carries the West Riverside unit, its shared owners and the EIA code 55641 of the older plant.
- recommendation: Keep the 2004 block as unit CC1 at this plant for now with Wisconsin Power and Light as its owner, and ask the GOGPT team lead whether the two blocks should be split into Riverside and West Riverside plants with their own owners and EIA codes.
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=True, contains_value=True)
  - links: https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/352541/000035254126000007/lnt-20251231.htm (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- notes: Open the federal file at https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx and go to the Operating sheet. Filter Plant ID to 55641 for the 2004 block and to 64020 for West Riverside. The plant-level owner list in GEM gives the West Riverside shares, which do not fit the older block. Alliant's 2025 annual report lists the Riverside Energy Center as a Wisconsin Power and Light station.

- finding: The EIA-860M table for September 2026 https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx lists generators 1 to 4 as natural gas combustion turbines of 48.5 MW, operating 1973, retired May 2025. GEM has no units for them.

### G100000403785 / Island Street Peaking Plant / FT81
*question about existing data*
- about: other
- question: Is there any document that states Backup Power for Island Street unit FT81?
- finding: The EIA table for September 2026 at https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx does not carry a backup power field. WPPI Energy's power supply page at https://wppienergy.org/power-supply/ lists the plant as a gas peaking plant of 52 MW but does not use the words backup power.
- recommendation: Leave Backup Power as no. The plant is a utility peaking plant on the grid, not a data center backup unit.
- notes: I searched the web for the plant name and WPPI Energy and found only plant directories that repeat EIA data. I did not open the WPPI page to confirm its figure.
